This is the hallmark of the lysozyme catalytic mechanism — Glutamate acts as the general acid-base catalyst (proton donor/acceptor).<br><br>Checking the choices:<br>- A. Aspartate — stabilizes intermediate but is not the primary proton donor/acceptor<br>- D. Lysine — not the catalytic residue in lysozyme mechanism<br><br>2. answer: A</td>
<td valign="top"><strong>Question:</strong> For the supplied protein, do not decide from generic acid–base plausibility alone: which amino-acid type is assigned the active-site proton donor/acceptor role?<br><br><strong>Choices:</strong><br><br><strong>A.</strong> Glutamate (Glu)<br><br><strong>B.</strong> Histidine (His)<br><br><strong>C.</strong> Aspartate (Asp)<br><br><strong>D.</strong> Lysine (Lys)<br><br><strong>Skills:</strong> active_site_0097:1<br><br><strong>Flash-Next response:</strong><br><br><br><br>1. reasoning: <br><br>Analyzing the protein sequence:<br>- The N-terminal region shows a signal peptide (MRKKLKWLSFLLGFIILLFLFKYQF...)<br>- Key motifs observed: &quot;DPGHGG&quot; and &quot;HLNA&quot; — these are characteristic of glycoside hydrolase family 18 (GH18) chitinases or related lysozyme-type enzymes<br>- The overall sequence architecture matches GH18/GH23 enzymes<br><br>For GH18 enzymes, the catalytic mechanism uses a &quot;substrate-assisted&quot; mechanism where a conserved **Glutamate (Glu)** residue in the DXXDXDXE motif acts as the catalytic acid/proton donor.
